...fixed sulfur <note>original: "sulphuri fixo"; in alchemy, "fixed" refers to a substance that remains stable and does not evaporate when exposed to heat</note>, to which the third part of the regimen <term>Regimen: the specific sequence of heating and cooling stages required to transform alchemical matter</term> has been entrusted, even unto the final degree, so that it may perfect the operation of the stone <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold and granting long life</note> infinitely, and persist with it, together with its own fire; and let it consist of a weight equal to the matter itself, in all things and through all things, without any variation in the degree of its transformation. 

Therefore, after the matter has been prepared and mixed in its proper proportions, it must be closed most securely in the philosophers’ vessel <note>The "vas philosophorum," typically a glass flask or "egg" designed to withstand long periods of heat</note> with its own seal <term>Hermetic Seal: an airtight closure, often made by melting the glass neck of a flask or using a specialized luting clay</term>, and committed to the secret fire. In this fire, the philosophical sun <note>a metaphor for the perfected state of the matter, often associated with gold or spiritual enlightenment</note> shall arise and ascend; furthermore, it shall illuminate all things that have waited for its light and have desired it with great hope. 

With these few words, we wish to conclude the secret <note>original: "arcanum"</note> of the stone, lacking in no point and without defect, for which we give immortal thanks to God. Now we shall unlock our Treasure for you—a treasure that cannot be paid for by the riches of the entire world.

56          THE TREASURY
The Treasury of Treasuries of THEO-
PHRASTUS PARACELSUS.

**N**ature produced one admirable kind of mineral in the bowels of the earth, and it is two-fold; this is found in various places in Europe. The best, which was placed into my hands and proven by experiment, is the star of the sphere of the sun, according to the configuration of the greater world <note>The "greater world" refers to the Macrocosm—the entire universe—which Paracelsus believed influenced the "lesser world" or Microcosm of the human body and individual minerals.</note> from its beginning. The other consists in the southern star; in its first flowering, it is a slime of the earth <note>original: "viscus terrae"</note>, brought into the light by its own star, and in its first *coagulation* <term>coagulation|the process by which a liquid or fluid substance becomes a solid or semi-solid mass</term> it is decorated with a redness. In it, all the "flowers" of minerals and their colors are included, concerning which much is found among the philosophers. Regarding its cold and moist nature, it has been compared and adapted to water. But the perfect knowledge of it, when brought to the test of experience, has remained hidden until now from many—indeed, almost all philosophers—as many as have shot their arrows at the mark before me. Striving in vain, they have deviated very widely from the true and complete target. They have believed that *mercury* and *sulfur* were the mother and father of all metals; but in truth, they did not mention the third <note>Paracelsus is famously credited with adding "Salt" to the traditional alchemical duo of Mercury and Sulfur, forming the "Tria Prima."</note>. Indeed, what is more, none of these men has yet been able to take the fisherman’s net into his hands, which is suited to the "true water," the mother of metals, which indeed—

<summary>Paracelsus describes a unique mineral substance influenced by the stars and the macrocosm, while criticizing previous alchemists for only recognizing Mercury and Sulfur as the components of metals.</summary>
<keywords>Paracelsus, alchemy, Tria Prima, Macrocosm, Mercury, Sulfur, mineralogy, Star of the Sun</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the chemical separation of substances, contrasting the new "spagyric" (Paracelsian) methods with the traditional medical teachings of Galen and Avicenna.</meta>

->OF PHILOSOPHERS, 57<-

Indeed, separated by the <term>spagyric art<note>A term for alchemy, specifically the Paracelsian method of separating and then recombining the components of natural substances to create potent medicines.</note></term>, it reveals its own "fish," which neither <term>Galenic fishing<note>Galen was an ancient Greek physician whose theories dominated medicine for over a millennium. The author is mocking traditional medicine as an ineffective way to "catch" the secrets of nature.</note></term> nor the <term>torn net of Avicenna<note>Avicenna (Ibn Sina) was a famous Persian physician. Calling his "net" torn suggests his medical system is full of holes or failures.</note></term> could ever catch. If I had to demonstrate to modern physicians even the mere names of **conjunction**, **solution**, and **coagulation**—which nature herself revealed and demonstrated in this creation of hers at the beginning of the world—a year would hardly suffice, nor would any amount of paper, however much, be enough to instruct them.

I say indeed that in this mineral, three substances are held: namely **Mercury**, **Sulfur**, and **mineral water**. From these, the mineral is composed; and when separated by the spagyric art, it is dissolved in its own liquid that is not yet ripe—hidden just as a pear is hidden within its tree. 

The concealer of the pear is the tree, in which the stars and nature come together at once. It puts forth green branches, and later in March, it reveals its <term>seeds<note>Original Latin: "atomos." In this context, it refers to the tiny, invisible precursors or "seeds" of growth rather than modern subatomic particles.</note></term> and flowers. They progress toward the production of the fruit and the pear until, finally, in autumn, the fruits emerge. 

In no different way should we judge the minerals in the bowels of the earth, which are pushed forth by the stars. This is something the alchemist, longing for nature’s treasure, must consider most deeply. I shall reveal the manner of this artifice—from its beginning, middle, and end—in the following treatise, together with its water, sulfur, and balsam. By the **solution** <note>The process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state.</note> of these three, and their subsequent **conjunction** <note>The alchemical "marriage" or reuniting of separated elements into a new, perfected whole.</note> into one, the whole business of the work is completed—

<meta>This page continues a sequence of alchemical instructions, likely from a "Treasury" or "Thesaurus" of chemical secrets. The text describes the purification and transformation of cinnabar.</meta>

18 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;**TREASURY**

...[it is done] in this manner. Take the ore of **cinnabar**, which you should wash with celestial water <note>original: "aqua coelesti"; typically refers to rainwater or dew collected under specific astrological conditions</note> for three hours; then strain it and dissolve it in *aqua regia* <term>aqua regia: "royal water," a highly corrosive mixture of nitric and hydrochloric acids</term> made from vitriol, saltpeter, and common salt. Let it be drawn off through an **alembic** <term>alembic: an early alchemical still used for distillation</term>, poured back in, and treated by **cohobation** <term>cohobation: the process of repeatedly distilling a liquid from the same solid substance to concentrate its "virtue"</term>, so that the pure may be separated from the impure, which settles below. Let it putrefy in **horse dung** <note>a common alchemical method for providing steady, gentle heat over a long period</note> for a month, and afterwards let the elements be separated, so that when they have revealed their signs, they may be distilled through an alembic using the first degree of fire, through which **water** and **air** ascend first, and finally **fire**, and so on through the remaining degrees, which can be discerned by an experienced operator. At the bottom of the vessel the **earth** remains, in which lies hidden all that has been sought by many, yet found by very few. You shall skillfully **calcine** <term>calcine: to reduce a substance to a powder or ash through intense heat</term> this earth, enclosed in a **reverberatory furnace** <term>reverberatory furnace: a furnace where the flame or heat is deflected or "reverberated" onto the material being heated</term>, proceeding from the first to the fifth degree of heat, spending five hours at each degree. This is done so that you may have a **volatile salt**, as subtle as alcohol <note>original: "alcool"; in early alchemy, this term referred to an extremely fine, purified powder or essence rather than a liquid spirit</note>, and a constant star of fire and earth, which you shall separate by the elements of water and air that you preserved earlier. Finally, place it to digest in a **water bath** <term>balnei maris: literally "bath of the sea," commonly known as a bain-marie</term> for eight hours, and you will see what has been unknown to many alchemists until now, and very little considered. Separate it according to your experience, skillfully and in the **spagyric** <term>spagyric: the alchemical art of "separating and recombining," often used in the preparation of medicines</term> manner; the earth itself will be rendered wonderfully white, from which the **tincture** will have been extracted. Join the element of fire with the salt...

It is truly wonderful. What, have your eyes been transmuted into glass and **carbuncle** <note>a deep red gemstone</note>, so that they only see those things which pertain to ornament and superficial spectacle, to pomp and pride? Truly, if your Doctors knew that their Prince (whom they call **Galen** <note>Claudius Galenus, the 2nd-century physician whose theories dominated medicine until the Renaissance; the author here suggests he is in hell</note>) dwelt in the Underworld, and had written back from there to this place, that...

->OF THE PHILOSOPHERS. 61<-

...themselves, so that they might openly display their well-deserved condemnation to the whole world, marking themselves with a fox's tail <note>original: "vulpina cauda"; a symbol of foolishness or deceit in 16th-century iconography</note> in their stupor. No less should be thought of Avicenna <note>A Persian polymath and one of the most significant physicians of the Islamic Golden Age; his "Canon of Medicine" was a standard university text that the author of this text famously rejected</note>, sitting in the porch of Hell, with whom I have had dispute and controversy regarding his <term>potable gold<note>Aurum potabile: a legendary liquid medicine made from gold, believed by alchemists to grant eternal youth or cure all diseases.</note></term>, as well as his <term>philosopher’s stone</term> and his <term>thériac<note>A complex herbal "universal antidote" used since antiquity; the author suggests the traditional version is ineffective.</note></term>. 

O you ghosts of sophists <note>Here used as a derogatory term for academic scholars who value rhetoric over natural truth</note>, who falsely claim the medical art, even though it was born from God and entrusted to Nature—not to you, most unworthy men, by whom it is unjustly despised. See for yourselves, you imposters of men, who love the high seats of the earth and sit upon them: after my death, my disciples shall rise up, who will expose you as hypocrites and bring you into the light, along with your most filthy cooks <note>A derogatory reference to the apothecaries or assistants who prepared traditional medicines</note>. You wretchedly seduce your Christian Princes and Potentates and lead them to death with your medicine. 

Woe to you on the final Day of Judgment! I hope that my <term>Monarchy<note>The "Monarchy of Medicine": Paracelsus’s term for his own supreme and divinely-ordained authority over the medical field.</note></term> will triumph with the honor due to me. Not that I exalt myself, but Nature exalts me. For I was born a physician from her; I follow her, she knows me, and I know her, because I have seen the light that is in her. I have proven it in the form of the <term>Microcosm<note>The concept that the human being is a "little world" that perfectly mirrors the "Macrocosm" of the entire universe.</note></term>, and I found it to be so in her world, which is the truth. 

However, to return to the beginning and to satisfy my disciples, to whom I am also imparting my doctrine in the best way...

<summary>This page contains a fierce polemic by a Paracelsian physician attacking academic medicine and the authority of Avicenna. The author claims a divine and natural mandate for his "Monarchy" of medicine based on his understanding of the Microcosm.</summary>

# 62 THE TREASURY

->...I strongly favor, provided they seek it in the light of nature <note>The "Light of Nature" is a central concept in Paracelsian philosophy, referring to the innate wisdom and divine intelligence found within the natural world, accessible through observation and experience rather than just books.</note>, and experience it, have knowledge of the stars, and are learned in philosophy: the nature of water, and everything I have written, will teach and reveal all things to them.<-

Take, therefore, as much of the mineral liquor as you wish, two parts of the salt of the red earth, and one part of the sulfur of the sun <note>original: "sulphuris solis"; in alchemy, Sulfur of the Sun refers to the essential "soul" or active principle of gold.</note>. Once placed in a Pelican <note>A "Pelican" is a specialized circulatory distillation vessel with two side-arms that feed condensed liquid back into the main body, resembling a pelican pecking its own breast.</note>, let them be dissolved and coagulated, and do this three times.

By this path, you will possess the tincture of the alchemists, the weight <note>In this context, "weight" likely refers to the "projection ratio"—how much of the substance is needed to transform a specific amount of base metal.</note> of which should by no means be described in this place, but rather in the book on transmutations. Whoever possesses one ounce of the star of the sun <note>The "star" refers to the purified essence or quintessence of the metal.</note> and projects it over several ounces of gold, will tinge <note>To "tinge" or "dye" is the alchemical term for the process of transmutation, where the "tincture" changes the quality of a base metal into a noble one.</note> its own body. If he possesses the star of mercury, he will likewise tinge the entire body of common mercury. If he has the star of Venus <note>Venus is the alchemical name for copper.</note>, in a similar way, it will tinge the entire body of copper into the finest gold, and into the highest and most constant perfection: the same is to be judged regarding the remaining stars of the metals, such as Saturn <note>lead</note>, Jupiter <note>tin</note>, Mars <note>iron</note>, and so on.

From these also, by the same method, tinctures are extracted, which are by no means to be described here, because they are contained in the book *On the Nature of Things* and in the *Archidoxes*. With these few words, I wish the first being <term>Primum Ens: the primordial essence or "first principle" of a substance, believed to hold its highest medicinal and transformative power.</term> of metals and minerals of the earth to be sufficiently declared to true alchemists, along with the tincture of the alchemists.

Nor is there any reason for any operator to be deterred by the span of nine months; rather, let him proceed without

--- Page 67 ---
66
->The Author’s Solemn Declaration<-

I would have willingly and more frequently abstained from the public disclosure <note>original: "propalatione," meaning to make public or spread abroad mysteries that were previously hidden</note> of the sacred mysteries of God. But what else is permitted, or indeed fitting to do, than to follow most obediently where the Fates lead? I frankly confess that in this place I act as nothing but a writer under the dictating divine spirit, and a scribe of its warnings, as if awakened from the deepest sleep. What can a wretched man bring forth from himself besides sheer ignorance? Or what does he have in which to boast, rather than having obtained grace from God, and being deemed worthy of His service and obedience—to whom alone glory belongs? 

May He therefore, who alone has the power and the will, grant that with such happy guidance and faithful companions, I may complete this journey I have begun, reaching that end toward which He Himself, by His own command <note>original: "nutu," literally a 'nod,' signifying the effortless execution of divine will</note>, wished to direct me. It is not to be doubted that this work is for the **Defense of the Truth** <note>original: "Vindicias Veritatis," a common phrase in 17th-century scholarship referring to the legal or theological restoration of truth</note> which has been oppressed by those who, until now, have falsely claimed it entirely for themselves. It is also for the liberation of those who were so blinded by such leaders that—though their physical eyes were wide open, their minds saw nothing. May they now see the light of Him everywhere, who is light itself and dwells in inaccessible light <note>A biblical reference to 1 Timothy 6:16, describing the nature of the Divine as being beyond human comprehension</note>: To whom alone be honor, praise, and eternal glory.

67

->PROLOGUE.<-

**S**upreme Goodness, eternal God, it can by no means happen that the human intellect—without the greatest consternation of its own mind—could consider even the smallest point of Your inexhaustible Wisdom. I shall remain silent on whether it could even be capable of expressing it with a faltering tongue, or writing it with a trembling pen, unless it be done by the command of Your Most Holy Spirit, who impresses that very thing upon the hearts of men. He never ceases to admonish them silently and exhort them daily toward the confession of Truth in all things. Since, if this is to be attempted or carried out by anyone living on earth, it is necessary above all else that he be illuminated by the light of Your divine splendor, so that he may acknowledge the darkness of his own ignorance and candidly confess that he can be freed from it by no means other than Your powerful hand. And if by chance, at the persuasion of the Slanderer <note>original: "Calumniatoris," a Latin term for the Devil, often used to emphasize his role as a false accuser</note>, he should seek help from himself, he will find himself submerged in a deeper pit of captivity; for by himself he will never find any other means of liberation than the snare in which he entangled himself from the beginning, when he turned away from the obedience he owed to You, his Creator. Most evident examples of this are daily set before our eyes through those things which, until now, the writings of infidels, heathens <note>original: "Æthnicorum"</note>, and Pagans have produced from worldly Wisdom—especially concerning the treatment of the faculties of the arts and

->68          MONARCHIA<-

...of all the sciences, in which they seek and teach nothing but paths deviating from the target of Truth. This very situation originates from nowhere else than from their own principles and foundations; they did not draw these, as they should have, from the true source of Truth—the center of all sciences—but from its opposite: the false center of lies, namely <term>Duality</term> <note>In this context, "Duality" refers to a state of being separated from the "One" or the Divine Unity. To the author, truth is a single point (the center), while falsehood is fragmented and divided.</note>.

And what is worst, they have striven most stubbornly to defend and fortify these errors of theirs against any pious and good men who try to lead the straying back to the center of Truth from God. To resist these men fiercely, they have built ramparts and fortifications, the better to hide and protect the falsehood of their poorly-founded principles. 

For example, that first and most powerful fortification of their error is this: 

**"One must not argue against someone who denies the principles,"** they say. <note>original Latin: *Contra negantem principia non est disputandum*. This was a standard rule in Medieval logic, meaning that a debate cannot happen unless both sides agree on basic starting facts. The author argues that his opponents use this rule to avoid having their own false starting points questioned.</note> 

Is it any wonder? It is crafted in such a way that if those principles are once granted, whoever has assented to them must necessarily fall into error and confusion. In this way, the "children of darkness" <note>A biblical reference to Luke 16:8, suggesting that worldly or deceptive people are often more cunning in their dealings than the "children of light."</note> are accustomed to ensnare unwary men and deceitfully trick them; they have always appeared more clever in their own generation than the children of light. Indeed, they learned the wisdom they possess from their parent—the serpent, no doubt. 

But when they are overcome by those who pursue the truth and hold wisdom from the center of the true, they quickly flee to slanders, calling those who [expose] their errors openly... <note>The text ends mid-sentence as it moves to the next page.</note>

<summary>The author argues that mainstream academic "sciences" are built on a false foundation of "Duality" rather than divine Unity. He claims that scholars use logical traps, like the refusal to debate their core principles, to protect their errors from being exposed by the godly.</summary>

# TRIADIS 69

...now they make them asses. O the insanity of men, who do not see that wherever they turn, they run headlong into the greatest disgrace. Would that we were adorned with the qualities of an ass, so that we might bear the slanders of these monsters—the mules and she-mules born between horses and asses—with patience. <note>In the symbolic language of the time, the "ass" often represented the humble, patient believer or the "philosopher," while the mule, being sterile, represented prideful but fruitless intellectualism.</note> Hence it happens that they prefer their burden-bearers <note>original: "baiulatores," those who carry loads, perhaps referring to worldly supporters or false teachers</note> to their own parents. This occurs because it is ordained by nature that, by a **magnetic virtue**, like is always drawn to its like.

Those <term>Ethnics</term> <note>original: "Æthnicones," a term for heathens or those outside the divine light.</note> and Pagans are not worthy to ride an ass with us, following the example of Him who is the highest Wisdom <note>A reference to Jesus Christ entering Jerusalem on a donkey, symbolizing humility.</note>; thus it happens that they have chosen monsters for themselves to ride. Truly, such a riding of monsters is not without its own prophecy. Why do we wonder if God sometimes rouses spirited horses—more impatient than asses—who are moved to kick against these monstrous offspring while they rage, so stubbornly driven into pride and ignorance?

If this race of men were worthy of the ass, they would certainly submit themselves to the **yoke of patience**, which requires neither bridle nor halter. They would imitate this very trait from the ass: that they would examine their steps, just as he does, slowly and humbly <note>original: "pederentim" (likely "pedetentim"), meaning step-by-step or cautiously.</note>, with patience, lest they fall into ignorance. That is, they would more diligently survey their foundations to see if they consist of solid stones, taken from the **Rock of Truth**. Furthermore, they will be forced in the future to come to the <term>touchstone</term> <note>original: "lapidem lydium," a stone used to test the purity of gold, here used as a metaphor for the ultimate test of truth or divine judgment.</note>, even if against their will, and the judgment of their own—

->### 7. THE MONARCHY<-

...at the end, it is with the greatest disgrace that we see and hear. Would it not be more honorable to candidly confess one's error and be led by repentance toward the **Center of Truth** <note>The author uses "Center of Truth" to describe a singular, divine source of knowledge, a common theme in 17th-century Hermetic and mystical Christian writings.</note> and the path of life, rather than to persevere so constantly in error along the broad road to ruin and perdition? To this end, we pray that Almighty God might deign to open the eyes of these men, and that their mind might become an "eye," so that, drawn by the Divine Spirit of His grace, they may return to the center of true wisdom. From that fountain, may they draw living waters, casting aside the spring of filth in which they have been submerged until now—and in which they still persist more obstinately the more they become known to men.

Good God, Powerful and Merciful, You know to what great dangers the sick are exposed due to the introduction of the <term>false medicine of the Pagans</term> <note>original: "falsæ medicinæ Ethnicorum." This refers to the traditional medical system based on ancient Greek figures like Galen and Aristotle, which Paracelsian reformers viewed as unchristian and ineffective compared to "spagyric" or chemical medicine.</note>, especially in this time, which is the most corrupt of all. In this age, it is clear that not only in physical medicine, but even in that which concerns the salvation of the soul, everything strives against the truth with all its might—but in vain. For through You we are made certain that whoever has been or will be ignorant of that Center of Your wisdom has never handled anything of the truth, nor will they ever do so. On the contrary, whatever they have attempted tends not only toward their own ruin and destruction, but also toward the ruin of those who follow in their footsteps. This is confirmed by Your word, as You assert to us through the person of Your Son, who says: **I am** <note>This is a catchword at the bottom of the page, signaling the start of the famous biblical quote "I am the way, the truth, and the life" on the following leaf.</note>

<summary>The author critiques the "false medicine" of the ancients and the spiritual corruption of the current age, urging a return to the "Center of Truth" found in God. He warns that following traditional "Pagan" methods leads to both physical and spiritual destruction.</summary>

### OF THE TRIAD 71

"I am the way, the truth, and the life." <note>original: "sum via, veritas, & vita"; a quotation of Jesus from John 14:6</note> From this we most firmly conclude that in all our actions, arts, and sciences, we must make progress from the center of truth toward health and life, both in this world and in the age to come. By the first word <note>i.e., "the way"</note>, charity toward our brothers is prefigured; by the second <note>i.e., "the truth"</note>, the foundation and end of our actions and works; and by the third <note>i.e., "the life"</note>, both forms of salvation <note>The Latin "salus" refers to both physical health and spiritual salvation.</note> are to be sought in the Center of Unity. We also have the most certain testimony, O Best Father, that You created the Physician and Medicine, and that for this reason the physician is to be honored—a point which no true Christian calls into doubt, provided the medicine is true and not false. The difference between these two can be sought nowhere better nor more exactly than from their own principles. Therefore, with God as our guide, we shall happily begin the **anatomy** of these principles from both sides in the following sections, starting from the Unit.

### Chapter One

**F**rom the beginning, before the Most High God procreated man as his final creature, according to his own Image and likeness, he willed to create man’s dwelling place <note>The "dwelling place" refers to the physical universe or the macrocosm created to house humanity.</note> out of nothing, and to place within it whatever would be of use to him. Nor did God wish this world to be devoid of his image, for he had established it as the first visible creature. This world, created in a most beautiful order and arranged by number, measure, and weight <note>original: "numeris, mensura pondereque." This is a foundational concept in Renaissance science and theology, citing the biblical Book of Wisdom 11:20.</note>, he adorned with truly admirable craftsmanship. He decreed to use these three instruments—number, measure, and weight—as the primary ideas and forms in this work of his.

From this, we conclude—neither unwisely nor rashly—that humans ought to draw the principles of the foundations of their arts and sciences with the greatest reverence and thanksgiving. This is especially true for the art of medicine (which was the first necessity that presented itself to man after the Fall <note>The "Fall" refers to the biblical expulsion from Eden, which early modern thinkers believed introduced sickness and physical decay into the world.</note>). We must do this primarily because we see that the supreme Creator used these numbers, measures, and weights, even though he had no need of them (for he, being Almighty God, could have created all things with a single nod); he did so, however, so that for us humans, who are powerless on our own, there might be examples...

<summary>This page introduces the divine origins of science and medicine, arguing that God used 'number, measure, and weight' as a blueprint for the universe to provide a model for human knowledge. The author emphasizes that medicine is the most essential science for humanity following the biblical Fall.</summary>

<keywords>Monarchy of Truth, Creation, Divine Image, Sacred Geometry, Origins of Medicine, Number Measure and Weight, The Fall of Man, Macrocosm</keywords>

->OF THE TRIAD. 73<-

[...that] He might give His glory, according to whose standard we might beautifully relate whatever we were to perform in this world, and that we might acknowledge all our wisdom as originating from the fountain of wisdom, God, and not from elsewhere, and that we might offer all received grace to Him. Therefore, from these divine likenesses, we shall teach our medical principles <term>medical principles: the fundamental laws or "first elements" of healing</term>, as is right, drawn and derived from them. We shall do this with the most powerful arguments and irrefutable examples, against which the Heathen and Pagan physicians <note>The author is referring to the followers of ancient Greek and Roman medical traditions, which were not based on Christian scripture.</note> so fiercely rage. In contrast, we have decided to set forth their principles as well, so that both may be open to the whole world, and it may be free for judicious men to discern between the true and the false foundation. 

We are especially comforted that we depend upon the fountain of Truth, and in this we imitate Christian Teachers and not the gentiles or the unfaithful; furthermore, our enemies can bring nothing against us except the authorities of men rejected by God and utterly ignorant of the truth that exists in God alone, whom they never knew. Let them show even one among their Teachers and wise men who has known God through the Truth, or who was not a blasphemer against God and the Son of God—such as that "Prince of Medicine," Galen <note>Claudius Galenus (129–216 AD), the Greek physician whose theories of the four humors dominated Western medicine for over 1,500 years.</note>—or one who did not stand as an idolater, and consequently, was not led astray by a spirit of contradiction. ->74 **MONARCHY**<-

...led by a reasoning which they are not ashamed to put forward so impudently: namely, that in the arts and in the professional faculties, nothing is dealt with concerning God. <note>The author is attacking the "modern" secular view of his time—likely targeting university-trained physicians—who argued that professional sciences should be treated as separate from theology.</note> It is as if they were to conclude that these fields should be possessed and practiced without God. What blasphemy this is! From this, it is clearer than light that their doctors do nothing out of charity, but only out of greed. Can there be a doctor or a medicine without its Creator?

But let us dismiss those men who even think such things, and let us come to the concept of the mind. God, certainly a pure and simple essence existing through Himself—incomprehensible by any other, invisible, indefinable, and indivisible—is represented to us through **Unity**. This Unity, being entirely free from "multitude" <note>In this philosophical context, "multitude" refers to the state of being divided or broken into many parts, which was seen as a step away from divine perfection.</note>, admits no opposition, and consequently, no weakness or infirmity.

Since the world was created by God in the image and likeness of this Unity, it remains in that Union, even though it is ordered through numbers (as mentioned in Genesis). Indeed, these numbers would still retain the nature of Union as long as they were under the obedience of man, and they would have produced no "multitude" or division, until man—seduced through the **Duality of the Binary** <note>original: *Dualitatem Binari*. In Pythagorean and Hermetic philosophy, the number two (the Binary) represents the first departure from the perfection of the One. It symbolizes the "Fall" into a world of opposites: hot/cold, good/evil, health/disease.</note>—plunged into the opposition and disobedience of the multitude. ->OF THE TRIAD. 76<-

...had fallen, deprived of all health. Since, therefore, the first origin of all diseases happened to humanity through our departure from Unity <note>In this mystical context, "Unity" refers to the original state of harmony with the Divine and the natural world before the "fall" into division and discord.</note>, it follows that all health consists in Unity alone, and must necessarily be sought within it. Now let us see into which diseases humanity fell: namely, into shame, and the horror of death, and the fear of both. Therefore, being unsound in both spirit and body—because humanity would depart into both deaths <note>The author is referring to the "double death": the physical death of the body and the spiritual death of the soul.</note> and, soon after the fall, would tend toward the destruction of both lives—a double medicine was likewise needed for salvation. This was namely the Mercy of God and bread. Under this latter term, the healing of the body is contained, expressed in these divine words of correction and punishment: *In the sweat of your brow, you shall eat your bread.* <note>original: "In sudore vultus tui, vesceris pane tuo." This is a reference to Genesis 3:19, describing the hardship of life after Eden.</note>

This "bread" is not only to be understood as that by which the body is sustained for nourishment, but also as whatever is necessary for a person's life. For a diseased and unsound body, the remedy for its life is bread; if it is deprived of this, just as if it lacked food, it becomes subject to death. Truly at the greatest cost, we have exchanged the "bread of blessing" for the "bread of sweat," of pain, of anxiety, and of care. 

Therefore, this "bread of medicine"—not the kind acquired through this art <note>The "art" likely refers to the practice of medicine or alchemy as a professional craft.</note>, but that which is necessary to recover health—is to be sought in no other place than where it was lost to us: namely, in the garden of happiness and unity, which we have undertaken to explain briefly.

76 ->MONARCHY<-

...we undertook. Previously, we touched upon the fact that the world itself was created in the image and likeness of **Divine Unity**. Inasmuch as its wonderful and absolute roundness—a pure and sheer simplicity without multiplicity, with which it was endowed from the beginning of its creation—becomes clearly known to us. For we see that a spherical body, however large it may be, and in whatever direction it is moved upon a flat surface, rests only on a single point, which it takes as its foundation; this point makes its hidden center manifest to the surface on every side. Within this, great mysteries lie hidden, both natural and supernatural, as well as the secrets of all arts and sciences. If, therefore, the world had remained in its **unity**, there would have been no need for a physician, nor for medicine. Therefore, the goal <note>original: "finis" — meaning the ultimate purpose or end-result.</note> of all medicine is to lead the sick person back to **Unity**, in which we have founded our principles of medicine. It remains for us to examine where our adversaries have drawn their own ideas.

## Chapter Two.

After Almighty God had created His angels and the ministers of His glory under the Union of His peace and concord, there arose—from an accidental fall <term>accessorio casu: a secondary or non-essential event not part of the original divine plan</term> least known to the understanding of men—the first among creatures, the confusion of the Pair <term>Binionis: the concept of the number two or the dyad</term> and of Duality, which is an enemy to God. This confusion, because of the multitude of dissension it assumed and supported by contrariety, transformed the created image of the Unitary into the Binary <term>Binarium: the state of being double or divided</term>, which is diametrically opposed to Divinity and fights against the Truth. 

Indeed, this "Two" became the devil and the slanderer <note>original: "calumniator," the root of the word "slander" and a traditional title for the devil.</note>, even by thought alone—namely, by the intent to build another Monarchy, one either equal to or superior to God. But no sooner was this crime of treason against divine Majesty <term>crimen læsæ diuinæ Maiestatis: the legal concept of lèse-majesté, or an offense against the dignity of a sovereign</term> conceived than the Binary, along with its accomplices, was cast out of heaven. It fell away from the light into the outer darkness and suffered the penalty of eternal death, without an end to its torment. 

To better explain this matter, let us bring forward the logic of the compass <term>circini: a drawing tool used in geometry, often used in Renaissance philosophy to symbolize God as the "Great Geometer."</term>, of which one foot remains fixed...

--- Page 79 ---
38 ->MONARCHIA<-

One foot [of the compass] remains at the center, representing the Creator, while the other foot travels the circumference, representing the creature. When the fallen angel attempted to imitate his Creator in the production of the Universe, he fashioned his own compass within his perverse mind. He was therefore forced to build upon a different center, because two centers cannot coexist in a single monarchy, nor can two circumferences.

For this reason, he is rightly called the <term>Binary</term> <note>The number two. In Renaissance philosophy, the number 1 represented the unity of God, while the number 2 represented the first step away from God toward division and "otherness."</note>, because he attempted to reduce the unity of the true center to a multitude of numbers. Having adopted a false center, he drew a circumference to the midpoint. When he reached that point, the foot of the circumference was again changed into another center, and he sketched another middle circumference similar to the first—one that enclosed nothing, but was interrupted, presenting a false figure. From this emerged the likeness of a beast and a double serpent, raising four horns toward heaven and creeping out against the true Monarchy.

Once the first crime was perpetrated—the root of all evils and all vices—this kingdom, divided against itself, fell along with its Prince, Lucifer, and his legions of fellow soldiers. We should not wonder, then, if God, during the Creation of the World (as recorded in Genesis), did not say on the second day—as he did on the other five days—these words: *He saw that it was good* <note>original: "Vidit quia bonum esset"</note>. This was due to the nature of that number's origin, from which multitude, confusion, division, and controversy took their beginning, as was heard above.

->OF THE TRIAD. 79<-

...was said to have happened concerning the **Binary** <term>the number two, often representing division or opposition in this context</term>. We do not, therefore, conclude that the "two" should be rejected entirely, provided they remain in **Union**, just like "two in one flesh" <note>original: "duo in carne vna"; a reference to the biblical concept of marriage in Genesis 2:24</note>. But whenever they are divided into a **Binion**—that is, they create a "double union"—we constantly assert that they are rejected along with the **Binary**, together with its offspring the **Quaternary** <term>the number four</term>; that is, the "four-fold" or "varied" state, generated from that which is doubly-varied. Whenever, therefore, two or more observe peace in the truth of unity, we say it is a well-constituted **Duernion** <note>a harmonious pairing where parts work as one</note>, and not a **Duality**. 

We must not think this of the **Binion**, or "double union," because there should not and cannot be two Unions, but only one; just as there is only one God, and there can be neither more nor fewer. We also do not wish the **Ternary** <term>the number three</term> to be interpreted as "tripled" <note>original: "ternariatum"; meaning divided into three separate pieces</note>, because it is the offspring and fruit of the **Unary** <term>the number one, or the Monad</term>, which never varied from Union, as the Binary did. 

To return to the matters we proposed to discuss: it was heard above that that divided Binary **Monomachy** <note>original: "Monomachiam"; literally a "single-combat," here referring to an internal conflict or "war of one" within a single entity</note> raised four horns, procreated by the birth of the Binary. The wise Heathens and Pagan physicians chose these for their foundation, by which they might tear **Union** into four, and thereby surpass the Devil twofold—for he only tried, in vain, to tear it into two. This is clear in the example where, from the **Single Humor** <note>The author is likely arguing for a single vital life-force or "humor," opposing the traditional medical theory of the time</note> which God willed to create and exist, they established **four humors**, persuading those who are poorly-guarded and unwise.

They do not see that wherever they turn, they find nothing except restlessness of soul; they find nothing certain, nothing true, and nothing stable. This is because there is no unity in their studies, but rather everything is contradictory.

I ask, should contradiction—that is, the infirmity of nature, or its weakness (which arises because of contradiction and the internal war of parts departing from harmony or unity)—be removed by war or by peace? A wise and prudent man would say by peace, not by dissent. For what is the difference between adding fire to fire, and adding disease to disease? 

It will not help them even if they respond that water extinguishes fire through opposition. These wretched people do not see, once again, that this occurs outside of Monarchy <note>Here, "Monarchy" refers to a single, unified governing principle of the universe or a "rule of one"</note>. For in a single Monarchy, there cannot be multiple centers, nor can a single space be enclosed by multiple circumferences.

Let these things be said regarding medicine for spiritual illnesses. Now we must turn to corporal and physical medicine, so that its Quaternary <note>original: *quaternarius*; the number four, representing the material world and the four elements</note>, by joining in a bond with the Trinity of the previous medicine, may both meet together in the Septenary <note>original: *septenario*; the number seven, signifying a complete or perfect cycle</note>. For the Quaternary takes the place of matter or the body because of the four elements, which represent the entire matter of nature, just as the heavens provide nature with every form and every action upon that matter.

Disease, however, is the impression of evil through the action of a "false center" upon a body that is predisposed to suffer an effect similar to that action. In contrast, Medicine is the repression of evil through the action of the True Center and the universe, into the body...

          ### Chapter six

To finally bring our principles into the arena against their enemies, we propose these theses for them to chew on. <note>original: "masticandas" — literally 'to be chewed,' a common metaphor in this period for thinking deeply about difficult concepts.</note> Unity in no way requires medicine, because it is health itself. Duality can by no means receive medicine, since it has been condemned forever without mercy. <note>In this mystical-medical context, Unity (1) represents God and perfection, while Duality (2) represents division, conflict, and the fall from grace.</note> Only the Ternary <term>Ternary: The number three. In alchemical medicine, this usually refers to the 'Tria Prima'—Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury—which were thought to be the three fundamental substances of all matter.</term> is that which can receive medicine, because it has been deemed worthy of grace through Him who alone is merciful. 

Why then do the Heathen <note>The "Ethnici" or Heathens refers to classical authorities like Galen and Hippocrates, whose medical theories dominated the Middle Ages.</note> doctors of medicine attribute to the Quaternary <note>The Quaternary refers to the 'Four Humors' (blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile) or the four elements. The author is arguing that this 'four-fold' system is fundamentally flawed.</note> that which can by no reason be squared with medicine? It is condemned along with its root—namely the Binary—from which it was generated; it is of the same condition and nature as its parents. For a good fruit is by no means produced from an evil tree. 

Therefore, let them continue to build their edifice upon this foundation, and protect it with a roof exposed to the air, winds, and the lightning of Jupiter, while we build upon solid rocks. We say and assert that there are only three primary substances in man—and not merely qualities, though they are well-qualified—in which, just as health arises through unity, so too does disease take its origin through Duality. Of which the first is heat—

The first is natural heat, as the root of life; the second, <term>radical humor</term><note>original: *radicalis humor*. Here used to mean a natural preservative that prevents the body from decaying or "corrupting."</note>, by which the former parts are kept safe from the corruption to which their nature is otherwise prone. In whatever body, therefore, these three come together through union, it can truly be judged healthy; but in whichever they do not, disease and the root of death have entered in first.

For this reason, we do not deny (as our enemies falsely charge against us) the <term>four elements</term><note>Earth, Water, Air, and Fire. The author is defending his alchemical views against traditional physicians who followed the ancient Greek elemental system.</note>; indeed, we approve of them much more soundly than they do themselves, since we teach that in each of the aforementioned primary parts, the four elements exist in a certain inseparable bond of nature.

The separation of the elements is utterly contrary to nature; it was by no means granted by divine power to nature itself, and even less so to men. Otherwise, nature would work toward its own destruction, which never happens; rather, it labors toward its own propagation through their constant commingling. By this very fact, those sowers of nonsense are refuted who prescribe the separation of the elements with a massive array of equipment, in any way other than by the removal of impurity from purity, while there remains nonetheless an inseparable association and natural
                                                               harmony.

Through this third element—namely, **Salt**—acting as a kind of middle ground between the other two (**Sulphur** and **Mercury**), she creates a tempered balance for the preservation of health.

It is clearer than daylight that for the joining of two things of different nature and properties—or those that are in any way opposites—there must be a "medium" <note>A middle substance or bridge.</note> that shares in the nature of both extremes. ->OF THE TRIAD.<-                                     95

...let us grant that heat, by its innate nature, tends toward dryness, while moisture tends toward coldness. Heat and moisture are the contraries to these qualities. When these are joined together in salt, they agree for the reason that salt is a **watery sulfur** <note>original: "sulphur aqueum"; in alchemical theory, this suggests a substance that contains the combustible principle of sulfur but remains soluble like water</note>. Let these few words suffice for the wise.

If anyone should ask us what salt is, we wish to respond most aptly: it is an **aqueous earth** <note>watery soil</note> congealed by heat, and an **earthy water** dissolved by cold. An unlearned person will stumble upon this stone again, saying: "It seems against nature for earth to be dissolved by cold—since it usually dries out—and to be liquefied by heat." This rash critic will thus confess that, until now, he has not truly understood the natural transmutation of the elements. 

However, so that he may have something upon which to sharpen his wit, we shall leave these and similar matters to be better chewed over, to be explained more broadly in another place, and we shall pursue our intended purpose. 

**Salt**, therefore, when dissolved, takes on the nature of water; but when congealed, it has the property of earth. If it should happen that earth passes into water, water into air, and air into fire, what shall prevent a return back to earth? Nature knows best how to observe this proportion with equal weights. 

We do not, therefore, deny the four elements, nor their qualities, but we do not confuse them as our adversaries do. Nor do we take them as the principles or foundations of medicine...

If anyone wishes to contradict these words, I call upon him to do so likewise with his pen, and by committing his works to the press, so that he may make these things known to the whole world, and that...

<meta>This page continues a philosophical and alchemical discussion on the nature of the "Triad" (the number three) and its foundational role in science and medicine. It transitions from a mathematical/numerical focus to a physical and elemental one.</meta>

->OF THE TRIAD.<- 99

...since the foundation and root of hidden things consists in this, it is with good reason that the natural foundations of our arts and sciences must first be drawn by us from here. 

What we previously achieved through numbers—under the concepts of Unity and the Triad—we now intend to bring forth by means of a center or a single point, enclosed within a monarchy <note>original: *monarchiam*; in an alchemical context, this refers to a single, sovereign state of matter or a unified ruling principle of nature</note> in the shape of a sphere. This "triangle," the first hidden body, is what we must elicit so that we may prove the perfection of our well-founded medicine. 

We must judge that the single body of the universe consists primarily of three bodies because a "one" can generate nothing from itself, except for a "three." <note>This follows the Pythagorean and Hermetic logic that unity is static until it projects itself into a trinity to create reality.</note> We say, therefore, that three things constitute one body through a composition made by the triple Divine Word. 

It is helpful to explain the component parts of the universe using different terms than those of our teacher, <term>Paracelsus</term> <note>A Swiss physician and alchemist (1493–1541) who established the "tria prima" (salt, sulfur, and mercury) as the building blocks of matter.</note>, in the following manner: 

We say the first is the **fiery substance**, containing within itself life and motion, or the "soul" of the elements. 

The second is the **airy substance**, which possesses the nourishment of life and the "spirit" of the elements. These two together have earned the name of "Heaven" and the "Ethereal Region." 

The third, indeed, is the **earthy and watery substance**; both together carry the "body" of the elements under the name of "Earth."

102          MONARCHIA

Now let us examine the foundations of our adversaries, which are taken from the <term>quaternary</term> <note>The "quaternary" refers to the number four, representing the traditional medical reliance on the four humors: blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile.</note>, by means of the <term>trigon</term> <note>A "trigon" is a triangle, here representing the author's three-fold philosophical or chemical system.</note>, so that we may see whether the individual centers of their triangles are reached by the circumference drawn under the center of union and health. Therefore, from an equilateral square, the triangles *a, b, d*, likewise *b, g, m*, and a third *d, m, K* shall be drawn, which include the triangle *b m d*. And under the center of this middle triangle, which designates health, let a circumference be drawn. 

We see that this circle does not pass through the individual centers of the remaining triangles; from those centers, straight lines drawn to the angles of the "triangle of health" do indeed constitute a hexagonal figure, but it is not a perfect one, nor is it equilateral. From this, it is to be gathered on the contrary that the medical principles of our adversaries, being founded upon the quaternary, are crippled by the <term>binary</term> <note>The "binary" or number two was often associated in Hermetic philosophy with division, opposition, and imperfection.</note> and possess no perfection. 

But if they should say that we have chosen triangles that are not equilateral: well, if they wish to be examined by similar means, it will not be appropriate to seek it from an equilateral quaternary, but from one that is unequal and imperfect, as is noted by *l, K, f, t*. And in this way the imperfection of their principles will be revealed, for they are taken from the imperfect. Whichever way they turn, unless they return to our equilateral triangle—which is born from a perfect spherical body—it is necessary that this quaternary offspring of the Binary must fall into utter confusion. But let them see for themselves whether they can escape ruin by any other way.

->106          MONARCHIA<-

One may gaze at the sun, but ears do not see. Unless these things come first, no one will be able to attain either the understanding of higher things or the proper and perfect operation of lower things.

Now, things—both universal and particular—as well as the conditions of things, are in some cases manifest, in others more manifest, and in some most manifest. Others are occult <note>occult: hidden or secret; in this period, it refers to the "occult properties" of nature that are not visible to the naked eye but known through study</note>, more occult, and most occult, both to the senses and to reason. The nature of things itself works this diversity. From this it also happens that some men turn out far wiser than others. Indeed, one is said to be more wise who perceives less concerning [mere] perceptible things <note>The author implies that the truly wise man focuses on hidden, intellectual truths rather than being distracted by the physical world of the senses</note>. Number consists of order and measure. Order likewise cannot exist without number and measure. Measure, in turn, consists of number and order. But Unity and the Ternary here do not admit number <note>In this context, it means they are beyond the reach of simple mathematical counting because they are absolute, divine principles</note>; instead, having cast off all multitude, they stand in the first degree by their innate and most simple purity. This is the way to the heavens <note>original: "superos," meaning the celestial realms or the gods</note>, through which the ancient sages, having set out with the intellect and the guidance of true reason, perceived many things that are beyond human grasp.

### OF THE TRIAD. 209

It colors all things. Simple Earth <note>original: "Terra simplex"; likely referring to the "Philosophical Earth" or the primal, uncorrupted state of matter sought by alchemists</note> is a pure and primary element. Proceeding from the One, it is not composed, nor changed, nor does it endure being bound, but remains that which is incorruptible. In the One, it consists as both one and not-one; it is not a number and yet it is a number; it is not counted and yet it is counted; between itself and the One there is no number.

In Unity it remains one, and through a joining <note>original: "complexum"; suggesting an alchemical or mystical union</note> it brings forth the Triad <note>original: "Ternarium"; the number three or a group of three principles</note>. Embracing this eightfold by a wondrous nature, it leads all things back to the One: its power cannot be fully explained by any of the masters. This thing is not the God we worship, for it is a created thing. It is an image of the human soul—neither living nor dead—through which, in every branch of knowledge, a marvelous effect is produced. I say this to you as a friend, by the highest truth of God.

Whoever has been elevated by the simple knowledge of this pure simplicity will be perfected in all natural science, and will perform wondrous works, as well as discover astounding effects. The simple Good is unique, and through it, not only similar things but even many dissimilar things are made. 

Compounded Earth <note>original: "Terra composita"; matter in its physical, "fallen," or unrefined state</note> is an element pure, simple, and unique by nature; but because it is compounded, it necessarily becomes manifold, varied, and impure. It is, however, reducible: through fire into water, from that into fire, and from fire back into the simple One. It is a number, and it is counted, and it is not a number, and it is not counted. It is not counted because it is simple by nature, yet compounded by <term>accident|In Aristotelian philosophy, an "accident" is a property that a thing possesses but which is not essential to its nature</term>.

Those who are ignorant of the depths of this connection wonder by what principles we use in the operation of wonders; they falsely believe we rely either on the help of demons or on some superstition against our most holy Christian faith.

There must be in him, with the light, fire; with the fire, wind; with the wind, power; with the power, knowledge; and with the knowledge, the integrity of a sound mind.

Whence all those I have seen working in **Natural Adept Philosophy** have either achieved nothing, or, after long and useless labor, have fallen into despair over vain, frivolous, and superstitious things. 

But the **Second Principle**—separated from the first in order, though not in dignity—which, being one in itself, creates the **Triad**, is that which works wonders through the **Binary** <note>the number two, representing duality or the relationship between opposites</note>. For in the **One** there is "one," and yet it is not "one": it is simple, and yet in the **Four** it is composed...

### OF THE TRIAD. 117

In **Adept philosophy** <term>Adept philosophy: A term for "high" alchemy or esoteric science, practiced by those who have attained a deep understanding of nature's secrets.</term>, for one who desires both to study and to work with success, the following things are necessary. 

First, that he be by nature not only inclined toward this art, but also disposed to it, or at least capable of being prepared through the instruction of a master, by means of a **rectification** <note>original: "rectificationem"; in alchemy, this refers to the purification of a substance or the soul.</note>, from the ternary <note>The number three, often symbolizing the tria prima: Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury.</note> into unity, divided through the binary. I am in no way able to explain this more clearly in writing, but I can do so in speech—though not to everyone, but only to those whom understanding has illuminated. 

Second, it is necessary to have a sufficient knowledge of language, so that the common tongue <note>original: "vulgaris"; the language of the uninitiated or the general public.</note> does not grasp such great majesty of this science of **Adept natural philosophy**. It is also very necessary to know the foundations of **Astronomical** instruction, or at least to have a friend who knows it at one's call. 

Third, it is necessary to have an abundance of books in this science, and specifically those that have been corrected—for it happens that very few such books can be found—or to have someone at hand who can correct the errors in such books, rather than increasing them. 

Fourth, a teacher most learned and most experienced in the art is necessary. Since this science is wrapped in such great mysteries, it cannot be grasped without a most skilled teacher; unless, however, Almighty God, by a unique gift of grace, should wish to illuminate the mind, which happens only very rarely.

122          ->MONARCHY<-

[...true] wisdom, which affects the one who knows it with great remorse and love; it does not puff up or make those it fills proud, but rather makes them mournful. This is according to the words of the Wise Man: *He who increases knowledge, increases also sorrows* <note>original: "Qui addit scientiam, addit et dolores" — a reference to Ecclesiastes 1:18.</note>, because in much knowledge, there is an equal amount of indignation. Indeed, we have need of great purity and holiness so that we may be able to obtain the fruits of true wisdom, alongside the most blessed apostles of Christ, in eternal happiness. 

Our God is holy, but we are sinners and unclean. Since, therefore, a union cannot occur between things that are dissimilar, how shall harmony be established between God and us? Let us hear Saint Augustine: *No one,* he says, *who is holy and just is without sin, yet he does not for that reason cease to be just and holy, so long as he holds fast to holiness in his disposition.* <note>Augustine is arguing that "holiness" is a matter of the heart's intent (disposition) rather than perfect, sinless behavior.</note> For the Scripture says: *The just man falls seven times a day, and rises again, but the wicked shall fall into mischief.* <note>original: "Septies in die cadit iustus, et resurget" — a reference to Proverbs 24:16.</note> 

If he falls, says Jerome, how is he "just"? And if he is "just," how does he fall? But he is not deprived of the name "just" who, led and drawn by repentance, always rises again. Not only seven times, but to the one who offends seventy times seven times <note>A reference to Matthew 18:22, where Jesus instructs Peter on the infinite nature of forgiveness.</note>, sins are forgiven if he turns to repentance.

->TRIADIS. 123<-

While the moon’s sphere is turned toward the sun, it becomes bright from the sun's splendor and is filled with light; and the more it is positioned directly opposite <note>original: "ex diametro" — referring to the moon being in opposition to the sun, which creates a full moon</note> and made visible, the more radiant it becomes. If the body of the earth should intervene, the moon suffers an eclipse, and is darkened and overwhelmed by shadows.

In no other way is our mind—when turned toward God through good will and the desire of internal love—bathed in the most beautiful and sweet illuminations of grace, and wonderfully strengthened in the sharp point of holy contemplation <note>original: "speculationis acumine" — "speculation" here refers to deep, prayerful meditation rather than guesswork</note>. For the grace of God by no means illuminates a soul already preoccupied with worldly or fleshly desires. Indeed, vain things can by no means be mixed with true things: the eternal with the fleeting, the spiritual with the fleshly, the lowest with the highest, the impure with the most holy, or celestial things with terrestrial ones, so that the mind might equally savor the things that are above in the heavens and those that are upon the earth. 

So that the mind may be completely reformed from its inverted nature, let us pray to God that it may become one in the love and knowledge of the one highest good: the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit. Having attained the grace of the Beginning <note>original: "principij" — referring to God as the source or first principle of all things</note> from which it falls away into the "multitude" <note>The "multitude" refers to the chaotic, fragmented nature of material life compared to the "unity" of God</note>, let it be reformed back to that Beginning by unity. This is done by reducing the <term>ternary: a set of three; here referring to the triple nature of the soul or the Trinity</term> to unity according to one's strength, and by restoring the mind to itself, so that a purer understanding may reach the goals rightfully set by the Beginning.

114          MONARCHIA

Truly and indeed, there is a means of arrangement <note>original: "medium dispositionis"</note> which is held to be a gift of God; for by this, the obscurities of the body can be removed, as well as the impediments that hold back the mind from reaching the place where it is drawn by desire and where true reason leads. Hermes Trismegistus <note>A legendary Hellenistic figure portrayed as a combination of the Greek god Hermes and the Egyptian god Thoth, regarded as the father of "Hermetic" philosophy and alchemy.</note>, a teacher of <term>attained natural philosophy</term> <note>The study of the laws of nature and the universe, often encompassing what we now call science and metaphysics.</note> and the most excellent among all, says:

"It is true without falsehood, certain, and most true by the kinship of unity. That which is above is like that which is below, and that which is below is like that which is above, because every number consists of unities alone, for the performing of many miracles of the one thing." <margin>From the Emerald Tablet of Hermes</margin>

Do not all things flow from unity, by the goodness of the One? Nothing different can be joined to unity, but only that which is similar, so that it may produce fruit through the simplicity and adaptation of the One. What else is born from unity but the <term>triad</term> <note>The number three, often representing a complete cycle or the union of opposites.</note> itself? The <term>unitary</term> <note>The number one; the Monad.</note> is simple, the <term>binary</term> <note>The number two; duality.</note> is composite, and the triad can be reduced back to the simplicity of unity.

Its father is the Sun, its mother indeed the Moon. The wind carried the seed in its womb. The Earth is its nurse. This is the father of all perfection in the whole world. Its power is complete and immense if it is turned into earth. You shall separate the earth from the fire, the thick from the thin. And the triad, now restored to itself, with ingenuity and great sweetness, ascends from the earth into heaven. And again, decorated with power and beauty, it shall return to the earth. And thus it receives strength...

For all the studies of mortals that are not directed toward <term>charity</term><note>original: "charitatem." This refers to "Caritas," the highest form of selfless, divine love for God and others.</note> are utterly vain and should be despised by every Christian man. 

Therefore, we must take care that our mind, not unmindful of its own salvation, never clings to worldly studies; it should use them only for common utility according to necessity, and not constantly. Finally, one must return to those things which lead to the knowledge of God through true love. Every art that does not cohere with divine science—no matter how celebrated it may be—does not remain in a person any longer than life remains with reason. 

Therefore, that science must be sought which remains even after death. It is that very science from which all others—even those necessary for the use of this present life—must take their origin and growth, and to which they must be conformed. One must see, therefore, in what order, with what study, and for what end everything ought to be known: 

**First**, in order, for the salvation of the soul. 
**Second**, in study, toward divine love. 
**Third**, in its end, for the glory of God alone and the benefit of one’s neighbor. 

The intention and the end are not one and the same for all those hurrying toward science and wisdom. Some...

<summary>The author argues that all human knowledge and "arts" are worthless unless they are rooted in divine love and the pursuit of God. He outlines a three-fold hierarchy for learning: the salvation of the soul, divine love, and the glory of God.</summary>

->226          MONARCHY<-

Some desire to know merely to satisfy curiosity; others indeed for profit; and still others for the pursuit of honors and high rank. It is inevitable that all such people are led into a depraved state of mind and various errors. But whoever desires to know so that they may build up themselves and their neighbors in the love of Christ, directs their labors toward the goal of practicing charity. The fruit of their work—provided their life corresponds to their teaching—is eternal happiness. For it is impossible for these people to go astray in their operations. 

Therefore, let all those who apply their minds to these studies take care that they do not approach with "unwashed hands" <note>A common Latin idiom (*illotis manibus*) meaning to approach a sacred task without proper preparation or a pure soul.</note> and an unpurified conscience; otherwise, they will find themselves merely "beating the air" and will incur the greatest disgrace, financial loss, and even danger to their lives. 

Those who have truly attained this most sacred philosophy from God must beware lest they "give the bread of the children to the dogs" <note>A reference to Matthew 15:26, meaning that sacred secrets should not be shared with those who would despise or misuse them.</note>; for if they do so, they should believe with absolute certainty that they will incur the wrath of the Most High Avenger. Let us look at what Libanius <note>Libanius Gallus, a mysterious wandering scholar who was the mentor of the famous occultist Johannes Trithemius.</note> wrote to his disciple Johannes Trithemius, admonishing him on this very matter:

"Keep your investigation," he says, "in silence <note>original: "anacrisim," a Greek-derived term for a preliminary inquiry or examination.</note>, and do not release the dove before its time. Leave the lying Phoenicians <note>The author uses "Phoenicians" as a metaphor for deceptive or greedy people, common in classical rhetoric.</note> behind, so that they may realize too late what is good. Beware, Trithemius, lest you lead the rays of the sun into caverns, for there are destructive dragons there. You should hide the more sublime air in the earth only once. Do not offer your bread to oxen and goats, nor [open] the spirit through the gates..."

...send out smaller ones. <note>The text begins mid-sentence, likely concluding a technical instruction from the previous page.</note> Be one with yourself, and take care that you are not alone. Use ants, if perhaps you delight in the catching of birds. Divide the light in water, lest it crackle when frozen.

To which the disciple replied: that he had spread straw for the oxen and goats, and that he had a great war with apes <note>In alchemical literature, "apes" often represented the "apes of nature"—those who blindly mimic the outward signs of the craft without understanding the true spirit or philosophy.</note>, but had finally emerged as the victor. And that he lives so much the freer, as he is poorer in the things of the world. These are the warnings and decrees of the sons of **Adept philosophy** <term>Adept philosophy: The secret tradition of the "Adepts," those who have mastered the Great Work of alchemy and spiritual transformation.</term>, by which they forbid pearls to be sown before swine. Meanwhile, if they possess any hidden secrets, whatever they may be, they attribute it all to God alone; and they communicate them to worthy "sons," whom they kindly exhort to remember to live not for themselves, but for God and for the children of God, and to give immortal thanks to the same: the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit, one God, to whom be praise, honor, and perennial glory.

->To the Kind Reader.<-

**E**very friendly brother should know that there are certain men who—driven by both ambition and (what is most foul) the desire for profit—do not fear to rob **Theophrastus Paracelsus** <note>original: "Theophrastum Paracelsum"; Paracelsus (1493–1541) was a famous Swiss physician, alchemist, and philosopher whose works were widely copied and often forged after his death.</note>, our teacher, of his honors and his labors. They rashly attribute his books to themselves, having scraped them together by any means or cunning from those who still held them as <term>manuscripts</term> <note>original: "manu scriptos"; literally "written by hand," referring to works not yet printed.</note> and had not yet published them to the light of day. 

But these wretched men do not consider that no vice is so hidden, nor can ever be carried out so cunningly, that it does not break out in its own time. Do they really think that the disciples of this man will not eventually recognize a deception of this kind? For these disciples will very easily detect his <term>phrasing</term> <note>original: "phrasim"; the specific style or "voice" of the author.</note>—even if it has been changed from his prose into verse, or masked in whatever other way—as well as his method of treatment, his specialized <term>vocabulary</term> <note>original: "vocabula"; Paracelsus was known for inventing many of his own chemical and philosophical terms.</note> (which cannot be changed), and other notable marks of this Author. 

Let them do and strive for what they can; this kind of malice will never succeed as they wish. Nor will it be able to protect them from bringing upon themselves as much shame as the glory of their vanity which they hunted and sought. Let it be enough for them to realize that if they scrape together great wealth by this path, they should at least leave the due honor to the Author, publishing these works in the name of him, who even in du- <note>The text ends mid-sentence with the catchword "du-", likely leading into "dubio" (doubt) or similar on the following page.</note>

Meanwhile, farewell; and best reader, look favorably and kindly upon our labors, which were freely undertaken for your sake.

132 ->ANATOMY<-

[Decorative initial P] Before we treat the anatomy of living bodies <note>In this context, "anatomy" refers to the chemical and physical analysis of the body's functions and fluids, not just the dissection of the deceased.</note>, we shall set forth the instruments which come into use for this art, such as measures, scales, weights, vessels, furnaces, and other things of that kind, each depicted for the eye in its proper place.

## On measures.

A small vessel made of tin or silver should be kept, of a proper capacity, which contains the fourth part of an apothecary’s measure. The urine <note>The analysis of urine (uroscopy) was a primary diagnostic tool for determining the internal state of a patient.</note> which it holds when completely full, when poured into a vessel hung from a balance, should weigh exactly eight ounces—and specifically those of the kind that shall be described hereafter. But if the patient’s urine were too little to fill this measure, let another vessel of smaller and middle capacity be ready at hand, and let the middle weight of the urine, namely four ounces, be examined.

Another wooden measure is needed, in a linear form, flat or square, which is divided into twenty-four equal parts by lines; as a whole, however, it should measure a sixth part of the length of a perfectly proportioned human body. This is to be understood in such a way that this wooden measure, repeated six times, makes up the height of a man. Consider it to be a foot-measure <note>original: "pedalem"</note> of twenty-four "thumbs" <note>original: "pollicum"; while a standard foot usually contains 12 inches, the author here uses a scale of 24 smaller units or "digits" to allow for finer anatomical measurement.</note>, so that our anatomical man <note>Likely referring to a standardized diagram or model used throughout this book.</note> has a height of six such feet, and one hundred and forty-four...

### Concerning the balance

The arms of the scale <term>balance (bilance)</term> ought to be crafted from copper or silver, not from iron, because of rust, which iron might more easily contract from the corrosive quality of urine. For that reason, if the arms were worn down in this way, the balance would be rendered unequal. The vessels hanging from each side should be made of glass, for the reason we have previously cited <note>Glass was preferred in alchemical experiments because it does not react with corrosive substances like acids or salts.</note>. They should be suspended by cords woven from hair, so that they absorb less moisture and also do not rot as quickly.

### Concerning the weights

The weights should be made of copper, in the shape of those used when gold and silver are committed to the scales. They should be of three types: the largest, the medium, and the smallest. The largest weights are suitable for weighing Mercurial urine; the smallest for Salt urine; and the medium for Sulphurous urine <note>In the "Paracelsian" medical tradition, all matter was thought to be composed of three principles: Mercury (volatility), Sulfur (flammability/oiliness), and Salt (solidity/ash).</note>. This medium weight should be equal to that which is used for gold; for indeed man is to be compared to gold, through a certain sympathy <note>In natural philosophy, "sympathy" refers to a resonant connection between different parts of the universe, such as the human body (the microcosm) and the metals or planets (the macrocosm).</note> which he has with it—a similarity which ought to be understood through natural philosophy. A drachm <term>drachma</term> of weight for our physical sulfur ought to weigh 60 grains of gold-weight. A drachm of our physical Mercury, 62 similar grains. A drachm

136               ->ANATOMY<-
